The body lives in\n   676\t// the ProgramSlot subprogram table; the dispatch loop advances by one after\n   677\t// this returns. Modeled on one iteration of run_foreach_per_iteration:\n   678\t//   inst.rate       = block_id (subprogram table index)\n   679\t//   inst.inputs[k]  = caller's argument buffer for parameter k\n   680\t//   inst.out_buffer = caller's result buffer\n   681\t//   inst.state_id   = per-call-site disambiguator (drives state isolation)\n   682\tvoid VM::execute_block_call(const ProgramSlot* slot,\n   683\t                            std::span<const Instruction> program,\n   684\t                            std::size_t ip,\n   685\t                            std::span<const Instruction> binds) {\n   686\t    const auto& inst = program[ip];\n   687\t    const std::uint32_t block_id = inst.rate;\n   688\t    const auto body = slot->block_body(block_id);\n   689\t    if (body.empty()) {\n   690\t        // Out-of-range / corrupt block \u2014 skip silently (dispatch advances by 1).\n   691\t        return;\n   692\t    }\n   693\t    const BlockEntry& entry = slot->blocks[block_id];\n   694\t\n   695\t    // Copy each caller argument buffer into the body's fixed convention param\n   696\t    // bank, so the once-compiled shared body reads its parameters from stable\n   697\t    // indices regardless of which call site invoked it. Now let me state my hypothesis.\n\n**Hypothesis:** With 8-lane AVX2 kernels already in place, the remaining cost is not compute or dispatch but the L1 data cache: the bench (and any real graph) writes 64 distinct 512-byte output buffers per block (32 KB), and because each buffer occupies 8 consecutive L1 sets (512-byte stride, 64 sets, 12 ways), output buffers with the same `k mod 8` class conflict-evict each other *and* the hot input buffers \u2014 so stores take read-for-ownership misses from L2 and the `a`/`b` loads get evicted mid-block. The single change: use **non-temporal (streaming) stores** (`_mm256_stream_ps`) for the output, which skips the RFO and the L1 write-allocate entirely, keeping the inputs L1-resident. Values are bit-identical (same IEEE-754 result, same store data; only the cacheability hint changes).",

No allocation, lock or syscall; the ISA check\\n// in the opcode reads a global written once at static init.\\n//\\n// AVX2 store policy: the *output* line is written with a non-temporal\\n// (streaming) store. Rationale: an elementwise opcode writes a whole\\n// 512-byte buffer it never reads back, and BufferPool buffers are 512-byte\\n// strided inside one alignas(32) slab, so every buffer occupies the same 8\\n// L1 sets as buffers 8 slots away. With tens of live buffers the outputs\\n// conflict-evict each other (12 ways) and the *inputs* too, so ordinary\\n// stores take read-for-ownership misses from L2 and mid-block the source\\n// buffers fall out of L1. A streaming store skips the RFO and never\\n// allocates in L1, so `a`/`b` stay resident for the whole program.\\n// Bit-identity is unaffected: the stored bits are exactly the IEEE-754\\n// result (a cacheability hint, not a different value), and a single core\\n// always observes its own stores in program order, so a later opcode in\\n// the same block (or the dump path) reading `out` sees the same data.\\nnamespace kernels {\\n\\n#if CEDAR_SIMD_X86\\n// AVX2: 8 lanes, aligned loads, non-temporal output stores.\\nCEDAR_TARGET_AVX2 inline void add_avx2(const float* a, const float* b, float* out) noexcept {\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(a, 32);\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(b, 32);\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(out, 32);\\n    for (std::size_t i = 0; i < BLOCK_SIZE; i += 8) {\\n        _mm256_stream_ps(out + i, _mm256_add_ps(_mm256_load_ps(a + i), _mm256_load_ps(b + i)));\\n    }\\n}\\n\\nCEDAR_TARGET_AVX2 inline void sub_avx2(const float* a, const float* b, float* out) noexcept {\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(a, 32);\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(b, 32);\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(out, 32);\\n    for (std::size_t i = 0; i < BLOCK_SIZE; i += 8) {\\n        _mm256_stream_ps(out + i, _mm256_sub_ps(_mm256_load_ps(a + i), _mm256_load_ps(b + i)));\\n    }\\n}\\n\\nCEDAR_TARGET_AVX2 inline void mul_avx2(const float* a, const float* b, float* out) noexcept {\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(a, 32);\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(b, 32);\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(out, 32);\\n    for (std::size_t i = 0; i < BLOCK_SIZE; i += 8) {\\n        _mm256_stream_ps(out + i, _mm256_mul_ps(_mm256_load_ps(a + i), _mm256_load_ps(b + i)));\\n    }\\n}\"}"

Reverting it and going for a structurally different change.\n\nKey insight from the vectoriser remarks: `statement clobbers memory: cedar::kernels::mul_avx2 (_13455, ...)` \u2014 the AVX2 kernels are **out-of-line calls**. GCC cannot inline a `target(\"avx2\")` function into the non-AVX2 TU hosting the opcode switch, so every one of the 64 dispatches per block pays a full call (argument setup, caller-saved spills, ret) plus a memory-clobber barrier that blocks scheduling around it.      "arguments": "{\"path\": \"cedar/include/cedar/opcodes/arithmetic.hpp\", \"old_string\": \"// Kernels may assume 32-byte aligned buffers (BufferPool slabs are\\n// alignas(32)) and BLOCK_SIZE is a compile-time multiple of the lane count,\\n// so each loop fully unrolls. No allocation, lock or syscall; the ISA check\\n// in the opcode reads a global written once at static init.\\n//\\n// AVX2 store policy: the *output* line is written with a non-temporal\\n// (streaming) store. Rationale: an elementwise opcode writes a whole\\n// 512-byte buffer it never reads back, and BufferPool buffers are 512-byte\\n// strided inside one alignas(32) slab, so every buffer occupies the same 8\\n// L1 sets as buffers 8 slots away. With tens of live buffers the outputs\\n// conflict-evict each other (12 ways) and the *inputs* too, so ordinary\\n// stores take read-for-ownership misses from L2 and mid-block the source\\n// buffers fall out of L1. A streaming store skips the RFO and never\\n// allocates in L1, so `a`/`b` stay resident for the whole program.\\n// Bit-identity is unaffected: the stored bits are exactly the IEEE-754\\n// result (a cacheability hint, not a different value), and a single core\\n// always observes its own stores in program order, so a later opcode in\\n// the same block (or the dump path) reading `out` sees the same data.\\nnamespace kernels {\\n\\n#if CEDAR_SIMD_X86\\n// AVX2: 8 lanes, aligned loads, non-temporal output stores.\\nCEDAR_TARGET_AVX2 inline void add_avx2(const float* a, const float* b, float* out) noexcept {\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(a, 32);\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(b, 32);\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(out, 32);\\n    for (std::size_t i = 0; i < BLOCK_SIZE; i += 8) {\\n        _mm256_stream_ps(out + i, _mm256_add_ps(_mm256_load_ps(a + i), _mm256_load_ps(b + i)));\\n    }\\n}\\n\\nCEDAR_TARGET_AVX2 inline void sub_avx2(const float* a, const float* b, float* out) noexcept {\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(a, 32);\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(b, 32);\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(out, 32);\\n    for (std::size_t i = 0; i < BLOCK_SIZE; i += 8) {\\n        _mm256_stream_ps(out + i, _mm256_sub_ps(_mm256_load_ps(a + i), _mm256_load_ps(b + i)));\\n    }\\n}\\n\\nCEDAR_TARGET_AVX2 inline void mul_avx2(const float* a, const float* b, float* out) noexcept {\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(a, 32);\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(b, 32);\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(out, 32);\\n    for (std::size_t i = 0; i < BLOCK_SIZE; i += 8) {\\n        _mm256_stream_ps(out + i, _mm256_mul_ps(_mm256_load_ps(a + i), _mm256_load_ps(b + i)));\\n    }\\n}\", \"new_string\": \"// Kernels may assume 32-byte aligned buffers (BufferPool slabs are\\n// alignas(32)) and BLOCK_SIZE is a compile-time multiple of the lane count,\\n// so each loop fully unrolls. No allocation, lock or syscall; the ISA check\\n// in the opcode reads a global written once at static init.\\nnamespace kernels {\\n\\n#if CEDAR_SIMD_X86\\n// AVX2: 8 lanes, aligned loads/stores.\\nCEDAR_TARGET_AVX2 inline void add_avx2(const float* a, const float* b, float* out) noexcept {\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(a, 32);\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(b, 32);\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(out, 32);\\n    for (std::size_t i = 0; i < BLOCK_SIZE; i += 8) {\\n        _mm256_store_ps(out + i, _mm256_mul_ps(_mm256_load_ps(a + i), _mm256_load_ps(b + i)));\\n    }\\n}\\n\\n// Inlinable AVX2 kernels (x86-64 GCC only).\\n//\\n// A [[gnu::target(\\\"avx2\\\")]] function cannot be inlined into the\\n// non-AVX2 translation unit that hosts the opcode switch, so every one of\\n// the 64 dispatches per block paid a real call (argument setup, spills,\\n// ret) and \u2014 worse \u2014 GCC models the call as clobbering memory, which\\n// blocks scheduling and instruction selection across the dispatch\\n// (vectoriser remarks: \\\"statement clobbers memory: kernels::mul_avx2\\\").\\n//\\n// These kernels emit the *same* AVX2 instructions the intrinsic kernels\\n// compile to \u2014 vmovaps / vaddps / vsubps / vmulps over aligned 32-byte\\n// operands \u2014 via an asm string, which needs no target attribute and\\n// therefore inlines into the caller. Single rounded IEEE-754 op per\\n// element, so results are bit-identical to the scalar body for every\\n// input, denormals/NaNs/signed zeros included (no FMA, no FTZ/DAZ,\\n// MXCSR untouched). \\\"v\\\" is a 32-byte-aligned ymm memory operand (the\\n// operand size forces the aligned form vmovaps; the register kind is\\n// ymm0; the clobber-less form cannot take a locking prefix, per the\\n// GCC \\\"x86 Operand Modifiers\\\" note on \\\"v\\\"). Ymm registers are clobbered\\n// as \\\"xmm0\\\"/\\\"xmm1\\\" \u2014 GCC's canonical spelling for vector registers.\\n#if defined(__ELF__) && defined(__x86_64__) && defined(__GNUC__) && !defined(__clang__)\\n#define CEDAR_ASM_AVX2 1\\n#else\\n#define CEDAR_ASM_AVX2 0\\n#endif\\n\\n#if CEDAR_ASM_AVX2\\ninline void add_avx2_inline(const float* a, const float* b, float* out) noexcept {\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(a, 32);\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(b, 32);\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(out, 32);\\n    for (std::size_t i = 0; i < BLOCK_SIZE; i += 8) {\\n        __asm__ (\\\"vaddps %[b], %[a], %[res]\\\"\\n                 : [res] \\\"=v\\\" (*reinterpret_cast<float(*)[8]>(out + i))\\n                 : [a] \\\"v\\\" (*reinterpret_cast<const float(*)[8]>(a + i)),\\n                   [b] \\\"v\\\" (*reinterpret_cast<const float(*)[8]>(b + i)));\\n    }\\n}\\n\\ninline void sub_avx2_inline(const float* a, const float* b, float* out) noexcept {\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(a, 32);\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(b, 32);\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(out, 32);\\n    for (std::size_t i = 0; GCC's raw ISA builtins\\n// (__builtin_ia32_addps256 etc.) carry no target attribute \u2014 that is\\n// exactly why <immintrin.h> wraps them in `#pragma GCC target(\\\"avx\\\")` to\\n// begin with \u2014 so a kernel built from them is a plain function and inlines\\n// into the opcode body (and through it into the VM switch), while still\\n// emitting the very instructions the intrinsics expand to: vmovaps /\\n// vaddps / vsubps / vmulps on 32-byte-aligned operands. i < BLOCK_SIZE; i += 8) {\\n        const v8sf va = __builtin_ia32_loadaps256(a + i);\\n        const v8sf vb = __builtin_ia32_loadaps256(b + i);\\n        __builtin_ia32_storeaps256(out + i, __builtin_ia32_addps256(va, vb));\\n    }\\n}\\n\\ninline void sub_avx2(const float* a, const float* b, float* out) noexcept {\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(a, 32);\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(b, 32);\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(out, 32);\\n    for (std::size_t i = 0; i < BLOCK_SIZE; i += 8) {\\n        const v8sf va = __builtin_ia32_loadaps256(a + i);\\n        const v8sf vb = __builtin_ia32_loadaps256(b + i);\\n        __builtin_ia32_storeaps256(out + i, __builtin_ia32_subps256(va, vb));\\n    }\\n}\\n\\ninline void mul_avx2(const float* a, const float* b, float* out) noexcept {\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(a, 32);\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(b, 32);\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(out, 32);\\n    for (std::size_t i = 0; i < BLOCK_SIZE; i += 8) {\\n        const v8sf va = __builtin_ia32_loadaps256(a + i);\\n        const v8sf vb = __builtin_ia32_loadaps256(b + i);\\n        __builtin_ia32_storeaps256(out + i, __builtin_ia32_mulps256(va, vb));\\n    }\\n}\\n\\n#else\\n// AVX2: 8 lanes, aligned loads/stores (out-of-line: needs the target\\n// attribute, which blocks inlining into the non-AVX2 caller).\\nCEDAR_TARGET_AVX2 inline void add_avx2(const float* a, const float* b, float* out) noexcept {\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(a, 32);\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(b, 32);\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(out, 32);\\n    for (std::size_t i = 0; i < BLOCK_SIZE; i += 8) {\\n        _mm256_store_ps(out + i, _mm256_add_ps(_mm256_load_ps(a + i), _mm256_load_ps(b + i)));\\n    }\\n}\\n\\nCEDAR_TARGET_AVX2 inline void sub_avx2(const float* a, const float* b, float* out) noexcept {\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(a, 32);\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(b, 32);\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(out, 32);\\n    for (std::size_t i = 0; i < BLOCK_SIZE; i += 8) {\\n        _mm256_store_ps(out + i, _mm256_sub_ps(_mm256_load_ps(a + i), _mm256_load_ps(b + i)));\\n    }\\n}\\n\\nCEDAR_TARGET_AVX2 inline void mul_avx2(const float* a, const float* b, float* out) noexcept {\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(a, 32);\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(b, 32);\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(out, 32);\\n    for (std::size_t i = 0; i < BLOCK_SIZE; i += 8) {\\n        _mm256_store_ps(out + i, _mm256_mul_ps(_mm256_load_ps(a + i), _mm256_load_ps(b + i)));\\n    }\\n}\\n#endif  // CEDAR_AVX2_GCC_BUILTIN\"}"
    }

  "content": "Dead end confirmed: GCC can never inline a target-specific function into a plain caller \u2014 the pragma region approach keeps the same single out-of-line call. Reverting to the accepted attribute form and making a genuinely different structural change to the kernel loop itself.\n\n**New hypothesis (the real one):** Per dispatch \u2248 60 cycles, but the 16 independent vector ops should take ~20 \u2014 the loop is strung out on a *false-aliasing dependency chain*: GCC cannot prove `out` doesn't alias `a`/`b`, so every store to `out+i` must be ordered before the loads `a+i+8`, `a+i+16`, serialising the stream on the 4-cycle `vaddps` latency (~16\u00d74 = 64 cycles \u2014 matching the measurement). The fix: restructure the loop into **groups of 4 iterations that load all 8 input vectors before any store** \u2014 this is safe even for the in-place wiring `out == a` (a 32-float group's stores can only corrupt values the group has already loaded), so it needs no `__restrict` on `out` and stays bit-identical for every possible wiring.",

Inside a group all eight input vectors are\\n// loaded *before* the first store, so the loads of the group's later\\n// iterations are not ordered after its earlier stores. Without the\\n// grouping, the compiler must assume `out` may alias `a`/`b` (nothing in\\n// the wiring forbids an in-place opcode), so every store to out+i had to\\n// be ordered before the loads a+i+8 and a+i+16 \u2014 serialising the whole\\n// stream on the 4-cycle vaddps/vmulps latency (16 \u00d7 4 \u2248 64 cycles of pure\\n// latency for work that is otherwise throughput-bound at ~20 cycles).\\n//\\n// The grouping is alias-safe for *every* possible wiring, including the\\n// in-place case out == a (or out == b): a group's stores touch out+i ..\\n// out+i+31, which can only corrupt a-values in that same 32-float window,\\n// and every a/b value in the window has already been loaded before the\\n// first store. The scalar body defines in-place elementwise arithmetic\\n// the same way (read both inputs, then write), so the grouped kernel is\\n// bit-identical to the scalar oracle for every input *and* every buffer\\n// wiring \u2014 denormals, NaNs, signed zeros included (single rounded\\n// IEEE-754 op per element, no FMA, no FTZ/DAZ, MXCSR untouched).\\n//\\n// `a` and `b` carry __restrict__ although they may legitimately be the\\n// same buffer: both are load-only, and reordering or CSE-ing pure loads\\n// can never change a value, so the promise is value-safe even when\\n// violated. `out` deliberately does NOT carry __restrict__: promising\\n// out \u2224 a/b would let stores move ahead of loads across group boundaries\\n// and break the in-place case above.\\nCEDAR_TARGET_AVX2 inline void add_avx2(const float* __restrict a,\\n                                       const float* __restrict b,\\n                                       float* out) noexcept {\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(a, 32);\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(b, 32);\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(out, 32);\\n    for (std::size_t i = 0; i < BLOCK_SIZE; i += 32) {\\n        const __m256 a0 = _mm256_load_ps(a + i),\\n                     a1 = _mm256_load_ps(a + i + 8),\\n                     a2 = _mm256_load_ps(a + i + 16),\\n                     a3 = _mm256_load_ps(a + i + 24);\\n        const __m256 b0 = _mm256_load_ps(b + i),\\n                     b1 = _mm256_load_ps(b + i + 8),\\n                     b2 = _mm256_load_ps(b + i + 16),\\n                     b3 = _mm256_load_ps(b + i + 24);\\n        _mm256_store_ps(out + i,      _mm256_add_ps(a0, b0));\\n        _mm256_store_ps(out + i + 8,  _mm256_add_ps(a1, b1));\\n        _mm256_store_ps(out + i + 16, _mm256_add_ps(a2, b2));\\n        _mm256_store_ps(out + i + 24, _mm256_add_ps(a3, b3));\\n    }\\n}\\n\\nCEDAR_TARGET_AVX2 inline void sub_avx2(const float* __restrict a,\\n                                       const float* __restrict b,\\n                                       float* out) noexcept {\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(a, 32);\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(b, 32);\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(out, 32);\\n    for (std::size_t i = 0; i < BLOCK_SIZE; i += 32) {\\n        const __m256 a0 = _mm256_load_ps(a + i),\\n                     a1 = _mm256_load_ps(a + i + 8),\\n                     a2 = _mm256_load_ps(a + i + 16),\\n                     a3 = _mm256_load_ps(a + i + 24);\\n        const __m256 b0 = _mm256_load_ps(b + i),\\n                     b1 = _mm256_load_ps(b + i + 8),\\n                     b2 = _mm256_load_ps(b + i + 16),\\n                     b3 = _mm256_load_ps(b + i + 24);\\n        _mm256_store_ps(out + i,      _mm256_sub_ps(a0, b0));\\n        _mm256_store_ps(out + i + 8,  _mm256_sub_ps(a1, b1));\\n        _mm256_store_ps(out + i + 16, _mm256_sub_ps(a2, b2));\\n        _mm256_store_ps(out + i + 24, _mm256_sub_ps(a3, b3));\\n    }\\n}\\n\\nCEDAR_TARGET_AVX2 inline void mul_avx2(const float* __restrict a,\\n                                       const float* __restrict b,\\n                                       float* out) noexcept {\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(a, 32);\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(b, 32);\\n    CEDAR_SIMD_ASSERT_ALIGNED(out, 32);\\n    for (std::size_t i = 0; GCC pessimises scheduling around asm statements (still `clobbers memory` in the remarks), so the removed call is worth far less than the lost instruction scheduling.\n\n3. **Load-before-store loop grouping** inside the intrinsic kernels to break the potential store\u2192load alias chain: bit-exact by construction (safe even for in-place `out == a`), but no measurable gain in the one (noisy) round it got.\n\nWith the 10-check budget exhausted and every idea either flat or a regression, the tree is left at the accepted 1.13\u20131.19\u00d7 state rather than committing a slower kernel.\n\nIDEA: out-of-line target(\"avx2\") kernel call + alias-ordered store/load chain per dispatch -> tried NT stores, GCC-inlinable pragma/asm kernels, and load-grouped loops; all flat or slower (0.50x\u20131.03x), so the accepted AVX2 intrinsic kernels remain the optimum reachable without compiler-flag changes (GCC cannot inline a target-specific function into the plain VM TU)"
